        Idea:
# Sprint 11A: Production Canary Code Patches

## Overall Context

This is the code-change slice of Sprint 11 production canary. RayWorker routes are promoted sequentially by selector, and any code changes during canary need extra care because there may be no comfortable rollback iteration once users are involved.

## Shared Operating Rules

- Promote by route cohort, not by broad backend switch.
- Cohort A holds 48 hours before B; B holds before E.
- Emergency rollback is a selector flip back to WGP; WGP remains selectable.
- Code patches must be narrow, reversible, and tied to live canary evidence.
- Do not mix runbook/dashboard-only edits into this milestone; those belong to Sprint 11B.

## Sprint Goal

Handle necessary mid-canary code patches without widening production risk.

## Required Deliverables

- Selector or worker/orchestrator code patches required by canary evidence.
- Focused regression tests for each patch.
- Updated rollback notes for any changed behavior.
- Evidence that active non-RayWorker routes remain green when shared app/completion/billing surfaces are touched.

## Exit Criteria

Each code patch has a concrete canary trigger, test evidence, and rollback path; no runbook-only dashboard work is mixed into the code milestone; WGP remains selectable for every affected route.

User notes and answers:
- Replan around an agent-owned endpoint validation inventory. Do not require the user to provide U1. Before any VibeComfy selector promotion or code patch, enumerate every app-used route/task type that can select VibeComfy, then require route-specific live RunPod evidence plus Reigh-shaped worker queue proof for each promoted route: create-task route snapshot, claim gate, worker execution with REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy, completion/artifacts, billing/task metadata when relevant, telemetry, and WGP rollback rerun. Current selector state only marks z_image_turbo as vibecomfy_supported; all WGP-only or vibecomfy_unsupported routes must remain unpromoted unless this evidence exists. RunPod/live validation should be performed by the agent, not treated as a pre-run user action.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 11A Production Canary Code Patches

## Overview
Sprint 11A should handle only narrow production canary code patches, and the agent owns gathering the evidence needed to justify them. The latest critique does not point to a different production root cause family; selector contracts, claim gates, worker/orchestrator lifecycle, completion, and billing remain the relevant code seams. It does show that the prior ordering was wrong: before any VibeComfy selector promotion or production code patch, the agent must first create an executable endpoint inventory and live-validation path. The current repo does not yet provide the full validation harness described by `docs/migration-vibecomfy-live-validation.md`, and the existing worker live-test matrix does not cover `z_image_turbo`, the only currently `vibecomfy_supported` route. This revision makes validation inventory and minimal harness repair Phase 1 gates, not user prerequisites.

The code shape is split across three repos in this workspace. Route selection and WGP/VibeComfy route contracts live in `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py` and `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`. App route stamping and task creation are under `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/` and app call sites that invoke create-task or AI generation flows. Worker claim and task-count request paths live in `reigh-worker/source/core/db/task_claim.py`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts`, and `reigh-app/supabase/functions/task-counts/index.ts`. Orchestrator selected-pool totals and stale-route lifecycle live in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/database.py` and `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`. Shared completion and billing surfaces live under `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/calculate-task-cost/`, and `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/billing.ts`.

## Phase 1: Evidence Foundation — Inventory And Harness

### Step 1: Build the app-used VibeComfy endpoint inventory (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`, `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Enumerate app-used route/task types** from `reigh-app` create-task and AI-agent generation call sites, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`, and `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`. Include direct image routes, edit/reference routes, travel/join orchestrated routes, child/control routes, and active non-RayWorker routes that share completion or billing paths.
2. **Record selector status for every row**: route key, task type, support state, selected template ID, selected profile behavior, whether it can select VibeComfy today, and whether it is app-owned, worker-owned, orchestrated, or non-RayWorker/shared-surface only.
3. **Treat current baseline as restrictive**: `z_image_turbo` is the only current `vibecomfy_supported` route; WGP-only and `vibecomfy_unsupported` routes remain unpromoted unless full route-specific evidence is gathered.
4. **Store the inventory as an agent-owned artifact** in the patch notes or a small repo-local validation artifact if implementation needs one. Do not ask the user to provide the initial canary trigger or inventory.

### Step 2: Make live validation executable before promotion (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Inspect the existing live-test CLI** and use it if it can already run route-specific Reigh-shaped worker queue validation with environment overrides. Do not rely on future-only scripts named in docs unless they exist.
2. **If current tooling is insufficient, implement the minimum validation-harness patch** needed to run promoted routes from this repo: add an explicit backend mode or environment injection for `REIGH_BACKEND=wgp|vibecomfy`, selector namespace/version/contract fields, and route/task selection.
3. **Add a `z_image_turbo` live-test matrix case** because it is the only currently `vibecomfy_supported` route and must be provable end-to-end before any selector promotion or code patch proceeds.
4. **Keep harness changes validation-only**. Do not change production selector behavior while repairing the validation path.
5. **Add focused harness tests** or smoke-safe unit coverage for any CLI/matrix behavior added, so the harness itself is not a manual-only assumption.

### Step 3: Gather route-specific live proof for every promoted route (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/`, RunPod worker logs, Reigh task metadata)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Run live RunPod template validation** for each route proposed for VibeComfy promotion, starting with `z_image_turbo` if no other route has full evidence.
2. **Run Reigh-shaped worker queue validation** for each promoted route with `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy` and route selector metadata set to the intended selector namespace/version/contract.
3. **Capture required proof per promoted route**: create-task route snapshot, claim gate with backend/profile/selector/contract fields, worker execution under `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy`, completion/artifacts, billing/task metadata when relevant, telemetry/log references, semantic or structural output checks, and a WGP rollback rerun of the same case.
4. **Record routes without proof as unpromoted**. They remain WGP-only or fail-closed and must not be included in selector promotion or canary patch scope.

## Phase 2: Narrow Production Code Patch

### Step 4: Choose exactly one patch boundary from the evidence (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Classify the patch** as exactly one primary boundary: selector map/route key, Section 3A support-report metadata, worker claim guard, task-count selected-pool visibility, orchestrator pool/stale-worker behavior, completion, billing, or worker adapter behavior.
2. **Reject patches without route-specific evidence** from Phase 1. If no route has enough live proof, stop after the validation inventory/harness work and leave production selector state unchanged.
3. **Reject dashboard/readiness/runbook-only work**. Sprint 11A may update rollback notes tied to behavior changes, but Sprint 11B owns dashboard-only and runbook-only changes.

### Step 5: Patch selector contracts with explicit mirror rules (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Keep WGP selectable** by preserving WGP-safe route contracts for unsupported, missing, malformed, or explicitly WGP-only routes. Do not replace cohort promotion with a broad backend switch.
2. **Mirror runtime route-contract fields** between Python and TypeScript when a patch changes route key derivation, support state, selected template ID, selected profile, selector namespace/version handling, route run ID, or worker contract version.
3. **Handle Section 3A metadata deliberately**: if the patch changes `SECTION3A_ROUTE_SUPPORT_MAP` `disposition` or `blocking_reason` semantics in Python, either add equivalent TypeScript support-report metadata and tests, or document in the patch why the metadata is worker-only and not part of app route-contract behavior.
4. **Add focused tests** for the exact patched route in `reigh-worker/tests/test_template_routing.py` and edge route tests near `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.test.ts` or a colocated `_shared/selectedRoute` test if TypeScript mirror behavior changes.

### Step 6: Patch claim and task-count behavior without overstating aggregate counts (`reigh-worker/source/core/db/task_claim.py`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/task-counts/index.ts`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/database.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Treat `claim-next-task` as the execution isolation gate**. Validate that claim requests and RPC arguments carry `worker_backend`, `worker_profile`, `selector_namespace`, `selector_version`, and `worker_contract_version`, and that mismatched tasks cannot be executed by the wrong selected backend.
2. **Treat `task-counts` aggregate `totals` as legacy/global visibility unless SQL/RPCs are explicitly changed**. Route-aware scaling should use `route_totals` plus orchestrator `selected_pool_totals`, not aggregate `totals`, unless the patch also updates the count/breakdown RPC signatures and SQL implementation to route-filter aggregates.
3. **If selected-pool counts are touched**, update or add tests for both the edge route-demand output and orchestrator selected-pool aggregation in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_route_aware_task_counts.py`.
4. **If aggregate route filtering is required by the trigger**, include the SQL/RPC migration or function change in the same patch and test that aggregate counts and selected-pool counts agree for the selected route while other route cohorts remain excluded.

### Step 7: Patch worker/orchestrator lifecycle only when live evidence points there (`reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Limit changes** to route-stale detection, selected-pool capacity accounting, stale worker drain/termination behavior, or route metadata written during worker spawn when those exact behaviors match the canary evidence.
2. **Keep drain-first behavior**: active route-stale workers finish current work; idle route-stale workers become terminable and are not counted as healthy capacity for the new selector.
3. **Test exact lifecycle behavior** in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tests/test_route_stale_worker_state.py` and any selected-pool scaling test that covers the changed capacity path.

### Step 8: Patch completion or billing shared paths only with non-RayWorker protection (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/calculate-task-cost/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/billing.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Touch these paths only** if the canary evidence is a completion/billing regression or the required selector/claim patch necessarily changes completion metadata consumed by these paths.
2. **Preserve idempotency and sub-task billing ownership**: `complete_task` should skip child task cost triggers where parent/orchestrator billing owns cost, and `calculate-task-cost` should remain spend-ledger idempotent.
3. **Prove active non-RayWorker routes remain green** with `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/tests/test_non_rayworker_fixtures.py` and relevant edge tests such as `complete_task/billing.test.ts`, `_shared/billing.test.ts`, and `calculate-task-cost/index.test.ts`.

### Step 9: Update rollback notes for the repo whose behavior changed (`reigh-app/tasks/canary-rollback-app.md`, `reigh-worker/tasks/canary-rollback-worker.md`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tasks/canary-rollback-orchestrator.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Update `reigh-app/tasks/canary-rollback-app.md`** whenever app-owned selector, route stamping, claim, task-count, completion, billing, or non-RayWorker route behavior changes.
2. **Update `reigh-worker/tasks/canary-rollback-worker.md`** whenever worker route selection, worker claim guard, adapter behavior, worker-local WGP/VibeComfy execution behavior, or live-validation harness behavior changes.
3. **Update `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tasks/canary-rollback-orchestrator.md`** whenever selected-pool orchestration, worker pool identity, route-stale lifecycle, scaling, or worker spawn route metadata changes.
4. **Keep rollback notes behavior-specific**: include selector env vars, worker pool identity, route contract version, expected stale-route drain behavior, and the emergency selector flip back to WGP. Do not add dashboard/readiness-only content.

## Phase 3: Verification

### Step 10: Prove validation inventory, harness, and production patch (`reigh-worker/tests/`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tests/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Verify the inventory** covers every app-used VibeComfy-candidate endpoint/task type and records `z_image_turbo` as the only current `vibecomfy_supported` route unless code changes intentionally alter that baseline.
2. **Verify the harness** can run at least `z_image_turbo` through the live route-specific path and supports `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy` plus WGP rollback rerun. If live credentials or RunPod access are unavailable after an actual attempt, record the failed command and block selector promotion or production patch execution.
3. **Run live endpoint validation first for promoted VibeComfy routes**. At minimum, prove each promoted app-used route through the live RunPod template path and the Reigh-shaped worker queue path. If only `z_image_turbo` is promoted, validate that route end-to-end and explicitly record that every other app-used route remains WGP-only or fail-closed.
4. **Run cheapest focused tests next** based on touched files:
   - `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest tests/test_template_routing.py tests/runtime/test_worker_preflight.py`
   - `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest tests/test_template_routing.py -k claim` when claim guard behavior is touched
   - `cd reigh-worker-orchestrator && python -m pytest tests/test_route_stale_worker_state.py tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_route_aware_task_counts.py`
   - `cd reigh-app && npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.edge.config.ts supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.test.ts supabase/functions/complete_task/billing.test.ts supabase/functions/_shared/billing.test.ts supabase/functions/calculate-task-cost/index.test.ts`
5. **Run non-RayWorker evidence checks** whenever completion, billing, app claim, app task-count, or shared app surfaces are touched:
   - `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest scripts/dual_run_compare/tests/test_non_rayworker_fixtures.py`
6. **Finish with the smallest broader suite** for each repo touched, such as `cd reigh-app && npm run test:edge:unit`, `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest tests/test_template_routing.py`, and `cd reigh-worker-orchestrator && python -m pytest tests` if orchestrator shared lifecycle logic changed.

## Execution Order
1. Build the app-used route inventory before selecting any production patch.
2. Make the live-validation harness executable before relying on live proof.
3. Gather route-specific RunPod and Reigh-shaped queue evidence for every promoted route; leave routes without proof WGP-only or fail-closed.
4. Choose exactly one production patch boundary from evidence.
5. Patch selector/route-contract mirrors before changing claim or scaling behavior.
6. Validate claim gating before using task-count results as operational confidence.
7. Patch lifecycle/claim code before completion/billing code unless the canary evidence is explicitly completion/billing.
8. Update the rollback note for every repo whose runtime behavior changed.
9. Run focused tests before broader suites.

## Validation Order
1. Verify the route inventory covers every app-used VibeComfy-candidate endpoint/task type, including app AI-agent create-task usage.
2. Verify the repo has an executable live-validation path for `z_image_turbo` and any newly promoted route; if not, the validation harness patch is required before selector promotion or production code patches.
3. Verify every promoted VibeComfy route has live RunPod template evidence, Reigh-shaped worker queue evidence under `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy`, completion/artifact proof, telemetry/log refs, and a WGP rollback rerun.
4. Verify the failing canary trigger, if any, is directly covered by a focused regression test.
5. Verify WGP remains selectable and explicit VibeComfy unsupported routes fail closed without fallback execution.
6. Verify claim responses and claim guards preserve selector namespace/version, backend, profile, and worker contract fields.
7. Verify task-count validation uses selected-pool `route_totals`/`selected_pool_totals`, or prove aggregate route filtering only if the SQL/RPC layer was changed.
8. Verify shared completion/billing changes do not regress active non-RayWorker fixture/oracle coverage.
9. Verify rollback notes include `reigh-app`, `reigh-worker`, and/or `reigh-worker-orchestrator` artifacts according to the changed behavior and identify selector flip back to WGP.

    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-156",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-164",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: route support states: the revised step 5 says to classify ltx control rows as vibecomfy_supported, wgp_only, vibecomfy_unsupported, new, or blocked, but the current worker routesupportstate enum at reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py:17-20 only supports wgp_only, vibecomfy_supported, and vibecomfy_unsupported. the plan needs to either extend the enum/report schema for new/blocked or keep new/blocked strictly as fixture disposition values mapped to an existing runtime support state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-9-control-rail-and-20260506-1802"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-153",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: worker integration: the migration doc describes the per-call profile override gap as involving reigh-worker adapter handling of override_profile before constructing vibecomfy sessionconfig, but the plan limits sprint 1 per-run work to the vibecomfy cli/runtime. that may be acceptable for a vibecomfy-library mvp, but it does not fully exercise the production-facing per-task override semantics called out in the overall context.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-155",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a residual issue-hint tension remains for resolution handling. the plan acknowledges current `vibecomfy run` lacks a resolution flag, but allows the adapter to leave resolution and other template-specific values unwired for sprint 2. because `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` are marked vibecomfy-supported direct routes, this may not preserve the existing worker task-parameter contract for tasks that provide a non-default `resolution`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-154",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting infrastructure for resolution is still incomplete if direct vibecomfy routes are considered production-supported. vibecomfy has a `vibecomfy.patches.resolution` helper and the cli can load scratchpad files with `allow_scratchpad=true`, but the plan does not require using that path; it permits leaving resolution out for sprint 2.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-162",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked vibecomfy profile wording against `vibecomfy/tests/test_ready_templates.py`. the revised plan says to add the production template to representative compile profile coverage 'alongside the existing dry-run id', but the dry-run id is not currently in `profile_smoke_template_ids`; only `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_5b_i2v` and `video/wan_t2v` are. this is a small wording mismatch rather than a functional blocker because adding either the production id alone or both ids would still satisfy sprint 4.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-wan-single-frame-and-20260506-0818"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked direct queue caller values in `db_task_to_generation_task()`: direct image tasks may carry `resolution`, `steps`/`num_inference_steps`, `seed`, and `override_profile`. the revised adapter plan covers seed, steps, and memory profile but not guaranteed resolution propagation, so it does not handle all relevant direct-route caller arguments for a supported vibecomfy direct route.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 8,
    "plan_count": 5,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-156",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-164",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: route support states: the revised step 5 says to classify ltx control rows as vibecomfy_supported, wgp_only, vibecomfy_unsupported, new, or blocked, but the current worker routesupportstate enum at reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py:17-20 only supports wgp_only, vibecomfy_supported, and vibecomfy_unsupported. the plan needs to either extend the enum/report schema for new/blocked or keep new/blocked strictly as fixture disposition values mapped to an existing runtime support state.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-9-control-rail-and-20260506-1802"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

  {

      {
        "id": "DEBT-014",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",

      {
        "id": "DEBT-110",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-013",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",

      {
        "id": "DEBT-108",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-145",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",

        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: worker integration: the migration doc describes the per-call profile override gap as involving reigh-worker adapter handling of override_profile before constructing vibecomfy sessionconfig, but the plan limits sprint 1 per-run work to the vibecomfy cli/runtime. that may be acceptable for a vibecomfy-library mvp, but it does not fully exercise the production-facing per-task override semantics called out in the overall context.",

  {
    "subsystem": "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them",
    "total_occurrences": 3,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-109",
        "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-146",
        "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-159",
        "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked direct queue caller values in `db_task_to_generation_task()`: direct image tasks may carry `resolution`, `steps`/`num_inference_steps`, `seed`, and `override_profile`. the revised adapter plan covers seed, steps, and memory profile but not guaranteed resolution propagation, so it does not handle all relevant direct-route caller arguments for a supported vibecomfy direct route.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
        ]
      }
    ]

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Idea:
# Sprint 11A: Production Canary Code Patches

## Overall Context

This is the code-change slice of Sprint 11 production canary. RayWorker routes are promoted sequentially by selector, and any code changes during canary need extra care because there may be no comfortable rollback iteration once users are involved.

## Shared Operating Rules

- Promote by route cohort, not by broad backend switch.
- Cohort A holds 48 hours before B; B holds before E.
- Emergency rollback is a selector flip back to WGP; WGP remains selectable.
- Code patches must be narrow, reversible, and tied to live canary evidence.
- Do not mix runbook/dashboard-only edits into this milestone; those belong to Sprint 11B.

## Sprint Goal

Handle necessary mid-canary code patches without widening production risk.

## Required Deliverables

- Selector or worker/orchestrator code patches required by canary evidence.
- Focused regression tests for each patch.
- Updated rollback notes for any changed behavior.
- Evidence that active non-RayWorker routes remain green when shared app/completion/billing surfaces are touched.

## Exit Criteria

Each code patch has a concrete canary trigger, test evidence, and rollback path; no runbook-only dashboard work is mixed into the code milestone; WGP remains selectable for every affected route.

User notes and answers:
- Replan around an agent-owned endpoint validation inventory. Do not require the user to provide U1. Before any VibeComfy selector promotion or code patch, enumerate every app-used route/task type that can select VibeComfy, then require route-specific live RunPod evidence plus Reigh-shaped worker queue proof for each promoted route: create-task route snapshot, claim gate, worker execution with REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy, completion/artifacts, billing/task metadata when relevant, telemetry, and WGP rollback rerun. Current selector state only marks z_image_turbo as vibecomfy_supported; all WGP-only or vibecomfy_unsupported routes must remain unpromoted unless this evidence exists. RunPod/live validation should be performed by the agent, not treated as a pre-run user action.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 11A Production Canary Code Patches

## Overview
Sprint 11A should handle only narrow production canary code patches, and the agent owns gathering the evidence needed to justify them. The latest flags do not point to a different production root cause; selector contracts, claim gates, worker/orchestrator lifecycle, completion, and billing remain the right code seams. They do show two remaining executable-plan gaps: route-specific VibeComfy RunPod template validation must be a real repo command rather than a future/manual script, and claim edge-function patches must run the direct `claim-next-task` tests. This revision keeps the inventory-first approach and makes the VibeComfy-side RunPod harness an explicit validation-infrastructure deliverable before any selector promotion or production code patch.

The code shape is split across three repos in this workspace. Route selection and WGP/VibeComfy route contracts live in `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py` and `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`. App route stamping and task creation are under `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/` and app call sites that invoke create-task or AI generation flows. Worker claim and task-count request paths live in `reigh-worker/source/core/db/task_claim.py`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts`, and `reigh-app/supabase/functions/task-counts/index.ts`. Orchestrator selected-pool totals and stale-route lifecycle live in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/database.py` and `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`. Shared completion and billing surfaces live under `reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/calculate-task-cost/`, and `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/billing.ts`. VibeComfy RunPod validation tooling lives under `vibecomfy/scripts/`; existing scripts can be reused only if they provide route-specific ready-template execution and artifact/log evidence.

## Phase 1: Evidence Foundation — Inventory And Harness

### Step 1: Build the app-used VibeComfy endpoint inventory (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`, `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Enumerate app-used route/task types** from `reigh-app` create-task and AI-agent generation call sites, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`, and `reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`. Include direct image routes, edit/reference routes, travel/join orchestrated routes, child/control routes, and active non-RayWorker routes that share completion or billing paths.
2. **Record selector status for every row**: route key, task type, support state, selected template ID, selected profile behavior, whether it can select VibeComfy today, and whether it is app-owned, worker-owned, orchestrated, or non-RayWorker/shared-surface only.
3. **Treat current baseline as restrictive**: `z_image_turbo` is the only current `vibecomfy_supported` route; WGP-only and `vibecomfy_unsupported` routes remain unpromoted unless full route-specific evidence is gathered.
4. **Store the inventory as an agent-owned artifact** in the patch notes or a small repo-local validation artifact if implementation needs one. Do not ask the user to provide the initial canary trigger or inventory.

### Step 2: Make Reigh-shaped worker queue validation executable (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Inspect the existing worker live-test CLI** and use it if it can already run route-specific Reigh-shaped worker queue validation with environment overrides. Do not rely on future-only scripts named in docs unless they exist.
2. **If current tooling is insufficient, implement the minimum worker live-test harness patch** needed to run promoted routes from this repo: add an explicit backend mode or environment injection for `REIGH_BACKEND=wgp|vibecomfy`, selector namespace/version/contract fields, and route/task selection.
3. **Add a `z_image_turbo` worker live-test matrix case** because it is the only currently `vibecomfy_supported` route and must be provable through the Reigh queue path before any selector promotion or code patch proceeds.
4. **Keep worker harness changes validation-only**. Do not change production selector behavior while repairing the validation path.
5. **Add focused harness tests** or smoke-safe unit coverage for any CLI/matrix behavior added, so the worker queue proof is not a manual-only assumption.

### Step 3: Make VibeComfy RunPod template validation route-specific (`vibecomfy/scripts/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Inspect existing VibeComfy RunPod scripts** such as `vibecomfy/scripts/runpod_validate.py`, `vibecomfy/scripts/runpod_model_matrix.py`, and `vibecomfy/scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py`. Use an existing script only if it can execute a named Reigh route's selected ready template on RunPod and emit route-specific artifacts/log refs.
2. **If no existing command provides route-specific ready-template proof, implement a minimal route-aware RunPod validation command** under `vibecomfy/scripts/`, for example `runpod_reigh_matrix.py` or a narrowly named equivalent. It must accept route key or task type, selected template ID, input fixture, RunPod target/options, and output directory.
3. **For `z_image_turbo`, wire the command to the selected VibeComfy ready template** and a small production-shaped fixture. The command must prove template execution, capture output artifacts, and record RunPod logs or job IDs.
4. **Keep this as validation infrastructure**. Do not promote additional selectors or alter production worker behavior in the VibeComfy harness patch.
5. **Add focused tests or dry-run coverage** for argument parsing, route-to-template resolution, fixture selection, output manifest shape, and failure behavior when required RunPod configuration is missing.

### Step 4: Gather route-specific live proof for every promoted route (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/`, `vibecomfy/scripts/`, RunPod logs, Reigh task metadata)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Run live VibeComfy RunPod template validation** for each route proposed for VibeComfy promotion using the executable route-specific command from Step 3, starting with `z_image_turbo` if no other route has full evidence.
2. **Run Reigh-shaped worker queue validation** for each promoted route with `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy` and route selector metadata set to the intended selector namespace/version/contract.
3. **Capture required proof per promoted route**: create-task route snapshot, claim gate with backend/profile/selector/contract fields, worker execution under `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy`, completion/artifacts, billing/task metadata when relevant, telemetry/log references, semantic or structural output checks, and a WGP rollback rerun of the same case.
4. **Record routes without proof as unpromoted**. They remain WGP-only or fail-closed and must not be included in selector promotion or canary patch scope.

## Phase 2: Narrow Production Code Patch

### Step 5: Choose exactly one patch boundary from the evidence (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Classify the patch** as exactly one primary boundary: selector map/route key, Section 3A support-report metadata, worker claim guard, task-count selected-pool visibility, orchestrator pool/stale-worker behavior, completion, billing, or worker adapter behavior.
2. **Reject patches without route-specific evidence** from Phase 1. If no route has enough live proof, stop after the validation inventory/harness work and leave production selector state unchanged.
3. **Reject dashboard/readiness/runbook-only work**. Sprint 11A may update rollback notes tied to behavior changes, but Sprint 11B owns dashboard-only and runbook-only changes.

### Step 6: Patch selector contracts with explicit mirror rules (`reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/selectedRoute.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Keep WGP selectable** by preserving WGP-safe route contracts for unsupported, missing, malformed, or explicitly WGP-only routes. Do not replace cohort promotion with a broad backend switch.
2. **Mirror runtime route-contract fields** between Python and TypeScript when a patch changes route key derivation, support state, selected template ID, selected profile, selector namespace/version handling, route run ID, or worker contract version.
3. **Handle Section 3A metadata deliberately**: if the patch changes `SECTION3A_ROUTE_SUPPORT_MAP` `disposition` or `blocking_reason` semantics in Python, either add equivalent TypeScript support-report metadata and tests, or document in the patch why the metadata is worker-only and not part of app route-contract behavior.
4. **Add focused tests** for the exact patched route in `reigh-worker/tests/test_template_routing.py` and edge route tests near `reigh-app/supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.test.ts` or a colocated `_shared/selectedRoute` test if TypeScript mirror behavior changes.

### Step 7: Patch claim and task-count behavior without overstating aggregate counts (`reigh-worker/source/core/db/task_claim.py`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/task-counts/index.ts`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/database.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Treat `claim-next-task` as the execution isolation gate**. Validate that claim requests and RPC arguments carry `worker_backend`, `worker_profile`, `selector_namespace`, `selector_version`, and `worker_contract_version`, and that mismatched tasks cannot be executed by the wrong selected backend.
2. **When touching `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.ts`, update and run `reigh-app/supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.test.ts`** for RPC argument mapping, selector/profile/contract forwarding, incompatible-pool no-claim behavior, and response fields used by worker guards.
3. **Treat `task-counts` aggregate `totals` as legacy/global visibility unless SQL/RPCs are explicitly changed**. Route-aware scaling should use `route_totals` plus orchestrator `selected_pool_totals`, not aggregate `totals`, unless the patch also updates the count/breakdown RPC signatures and SQL implementation to route-filter aggregates.
4. **If selected-pool counts are touched**, update or add tests for both the edge route-demand output and orchestrator selected-pool aggregation in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_route_aware_task_counts.py`.
5. **If aggregate route filtering is required by the trigger**, include the SQL/RPC migration or function change in the same patch and test that aggregate counts and selected-pool counts agree for the selected route while other route cohorts remain excluded.

### Step 8: Patch worker/orchestrator lifecycle only when live evidence points there (`reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Limit changes** to route-stale detection, selected-pool capacity accounting, stale worker drain/termination behavior, or route metadata written during worker spawn when those exact behaviors match the canary evidence.
2. **Keep drain-first behavior**: active route-stale workers finish current work; idle route-stale workers become terminable and are not counted as healthy capacity for the new selector.
3. **Test exact lifecycle behavior** in `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tests/test_route_stale_worker_state.py` and any selected-pool scaling test that covers the changed capacity path.

### Step 9: Patch completion or billing shared paths only with non-RayWorker protection (`reigh-app/supabase/functions/complete_task/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/calculate-task-cost/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/_shared/billing.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Touch these paths only** if the canary evidence is a completion/billing regression or the required selector/claim patch necessarily changes completion metadata consumed by these paths.
2. **Preserve idempotency and sub-task billing ownership**: `complete_task` should skip child task cost triggers where parent/orchestrator billing owns cost, and `calculate-task-cost` should remain spend-ledger idempotent.
3. **Prove active non-RayWorker routes remain green** with `reigh-worker/scripts/dual_run_compare/tests/test_non_rayworker_fixtures.py` and relevant edge tests such as `complete_task/billing.test.ts`, `_shared/billing.test.ts`, and `calculate-task-cost/index.test.ts`.

### Step 10: Update rollback notes for the repo whose behavior changed (`reigh-app/tasks/canary-rollback-app.md`, `reigh-worker/tasks/canary-rollback-worker.md`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tasks/canary-rollback-orchestrator.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Update `reigh-app/tasks/canary-rollback-app.md`** whenever app-owned selector, route stamping, claim, task-count, completion, billing, or non-RayWorker route behavior changes.
2. **Update `reigh-worker/tasks/canary-rollback-worker.md`** whenever worker route selection, worker claim guard, adapter behavior, worker-local WGP/VibeComfy execution behavior, or live-validation harness behavior changes.
3. **Update `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tasks/canary-rollback-orchestrator.md`** whenever selected-pool orchestration, worker pool identity, route-stale lifecycle, scaling, or worker spawn route metadata changes.
4. **Keep rollback notes behavior-specific**: include selector env vars, worker pool identity, route contract version, expected stale-route drain behavior, and the emergency selector flip back to WGP. Do not add dashboard/readiness-only content.

## Phase 3: Verification

### Step 11: Prove validation inventory, harness, and production patch (`reigh-worker/tests/`, `vibecomfy/scripts/`, `reigh-worker-orchestrator/tests/`, `reigh-app/supabase/functions/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Verify the inventory** covers every app-used VibeComfy-candidate endpoint/task type and records `z_image_turbo` as the only current `vibecomfy_supported` route unless code changes intentionally alter that baseline.
2. **Verify the worker queue harness** can run at least `z_image_turbo` through the Reigh-shaped route-specific path and supports `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy` plus WGP rollback rerun.
3. **Verify the VibeComfy RunPod template harness** can run at least `z_image_turbo` through a route-specific selected-template command and emit output artifacts plus RunPod log/job references. If live credentials or RunPod access are unavailable after an actual command attempt, record the failed command and block selector promotion or production patch execution.
4. **Run live endpoint validation first for promoted VibeComfy routes**. At minimum, prove each promoted app-used route through the live VibeComfy RunPod template path and the Reigh-shaped worker queue path. If only `z_image_turbo` is promoted, validate that route end-to-end and explicitly record that every other app-used route remains WGP-only or fail-closed.
5. **Run cheapest focused tests next** based on touched files:
   - `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest tests/test_template_routing.py tests/runtime/test_worker_preflight.py`
   - `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest tests/test_template_routing.py -k claim` when worker-side claim guard behavior is touched
   - `cd reigh-worker-orchestrator && python -m pytest tests/test_route_stale_worker_state.py tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_route_aware_task_counts.py`
   - `cd reigh-app && npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.edge.config.ts supabase/functions/create-task/routeContract.test.ts supabase/functions/claim-next-task/index.test.ts supabase/functions/complete_task/billing.test.ts supabase/functions/_shared/billing.test.ts supabase/functions/calculate-task-cost/index.test.ts`
6. **Run `claim-next-task` edge tests whenever claim edge behavior is touched**, even if worker-side claim tests pass, because worker tests do not prove edge RPC argument mapping or no-claim response behavior.
7. **Run non-RayWorker evidence checks** whenever completion, billing, app claim, app task-count, or shared app surfaces are touched:
   - `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest scripts/dual_run_compare/tests/test_non_rayworker_fixtures.py`
8. **Finish with the smallest broader suite** for each repo touched, such as `cd reigh-app && npm run test:edge:unit`, `cd reigh-worker && python -m pytest tests/test_template_routing.py`, and `cd reigh-worker-orchestrator && python -m pytest tests` if orchestrator shared lifecycle logic changed.

## Execution Order
1. Build the app-used route inventory before selecting any production patch.
2. Make both validation harnesses executable: Reigh-shaped worker queue validation and VibeComfy RunPod route-template validation.
3. Gather route-specific RunPod template and Reigh-shaped queue evidence for every promoted route; leave routes without proof WGP-only or fail-closed.
4. Choose exactly one production patch boundary from evidence.
5. Patch selector/route-contract mirrors before changing claim or scaling behavior.
6. Validate claim gating before using task-count results as operational confidence.
7. Patch lifecycle/claim code before completion/billing code unless the canary evidence is explicitly completion/billing.
8. Update the rollback note for every repo whose runtime behavior changed.
9. Run focused tests before broader suites.

## Validation Order
1. Verify the route inventory covers every app-used VibeComfy-candidate endpoint/task type, including app AI-agent create-task usage.
2. Verify the repo has executable live-validation paths for `z_image_turbo` and any newly promoted route on both sides: VibeComfy RunPod selected-template execution and Reigh-shaped worker queue execution.
3. Verify every promoted VibeComfy route has live RunPod template evidence, Reigh-shaped worker queue evidence under `REIGH_BACKEND=vibecomfy`, completion/artifact proof, telemetry/log refs, and a WGP rollback rerun.
4. Verify the failing canary trigger, if any, is directly covered by a focused regression test.
5. Verify WGP remains selectable and explicit VibeComfy unsupported routes fail closed without fallback execution.
6. Verify claim responses and claim guards preserve selector namespace/version, backend, profile, and worker contract fields, including direct `claim-next-task/index.test.ts` coverage for edge changes.
7. Verify task-count validation uses selected-pool `route_totals`/`selected_pool_totals`, or prove aggregate route filtering only if the SQL/RPC layer was changed.
8. Verify shared completion/billing changes do not regress active non-RayWorker fixture/oracle coverage.
9. Verify rollback notes include `reigh-app`, `reigh-worker`, and/or `reigh-worker-orchestrator` artifacts according to the changed behavior and identify selector flip back to WGP.

        "priority": "must",

        "priority": "must",

          "parse_diff",
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {

        "priority": "must",

        ]
      },
      {

        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [

          "parse_diff"
        ]
      },
      {

        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "read_files",
          "parse_diff"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Focused regression tests cover each changed selector, validation harness, worker/orchestrator, completion, or billing behavior.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "parse_diff"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "If shared completion, task-count, claim, or billing surfaces are touched, active non-RayWorker route fixture/oracle checks still pass.",
        "priority": "must",

        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "read_files",
